{
  "text": "## EMNLP 2026 Mock Review — Soundness / Novelty / Presentation / Claim-Evidence / Missing Baselines\n\n**模拟审稿人背景**: 熟悉 model collapse 文献和 EWS 理论的 NLP 研究者。\n\n---\n\n### Dimension 1: Soundness（证据链完整性）\n\n**F1. [severity: warning] Figure 2 caption Type I 标签与正文矛盾**\n位置: experiments.tex L121\nFigure 2 caption 写 \"Type I (r=0): monotonic collapse\"。但 L125-126 正文明确说 Type I \"SLV exhibits large decompression (+97 points at Gen 1, peaking at Gen 2) followed by accelerating contraction\"——即 Type I 包含一个先升后降的 decompression 相位，并非从 Gen 0 开始单调下降。\"monotonic collapse\" 与正文矛盾，审稿人会抓住这个不一致。\n**建议**: 改为 \"Type I (r=0): decompression then monotonic collapse\" 或 \"Type I (r=0): collapse after decompression peak\"。\n\n**F2. [severity: warning] 三阶段相位边界缺乏形式化判据**\n位置: experiments.tex L44\n\"We identify phase boundaries at inflection points where r_eff and SLV transition from rising to declining.\" 这实质上等于\"相位边界 = 峰值所在代\"，是描述性的而非形式化的判据。审稿人可能问：三阶段是一个结构性现象还是仅仅描述了\"曲线先升后降\"？\n**建议**: 加一句强化论证：\"The consistency of the peak position (Gen 2 across all three seeds) and its coincidence with the qualitative reversal of σ₁ (from declining to rising) suggest that the phase boundary reflects a structural transition rather than a statistical artifact.\" 这不需要新实验，只需要更好地论证已有数据。\n\n**F3. [severity: info] \"Any per-generation reactive controller\" 泛化范围**\n位置: conclusion.tex L8, method.tex L39-40\n论文证明了 rate-based controller（监控 Δ_t）在 r ≥ r_crit 时必然静默。但 level-based controller（监控 SLV 绝对值是否低于阈值）不受此论证覆盖。当前措辞 \"any per-generation reactive controller based on these indicators\" 已有适当限定，但如果审稿人问 \"为什么不用 level-based trigger？\"，论文需要准备回答：在 Type III 下 SLV 始终高于 baseline，level trigger 也不会触发——但在 Type II 下 SLV 短暂低于 baseline（Gen 1-2），level trigger 可以捕获。论文未讨论这一情况。\n**建议**: 在 §4.4 或 Conclusion 加一句：a level-based trigger keyed to baseline SLV could detect the transient dip in Type II trajectories, but would not activate under Type III where SLV exceeds baseline at all generations。\n\n---\n\n### Dimension 2: Novelty Positioning vs SIGMA\n\n**F4. [severity: warning] \"order of magnitude\" 数值不准确**\n位置: related_work.tex L16\n\"an order of magnitude larger than OPT-125M\"。Pythia-410M / OPT-125M = 3.3×，Pythia-1B / OPT-125M = 8×。两者均未达 10×。审稿人（尤其有定量背景的）会质疑这个表述的准确性。\n**建议**: 改为 \"3--8× larger\" 或 \"several times larger\"。这是纯文本修改，不依赖实验。\n\n**F5. [severity: warning] 未显式利用 OPT→Pythia 的隐性跨架构证据**\n位置: related_work.tex L14-20, introduction.tex L14\nSIGMA 在 OPT-125M 上观察到 spectral changes，本文在 Pythia-410M（GPT-NeoX 架构）上确认。这构成了隐性的跨架构验证，但论文未明确指出。审稿人问 \"why only Pythia, how do you know this generalizes?\" 时，这是一个现成的防御点。\n**建议**: 在 related_work.tex SIGMA 对比段末尾加一句：\"That SIGMA observed analogous spectral shifts on OPT-125M (a different architecture family) provides preliminary cross-architecture evidence for the generality of spectral collapse dynamics.\"\n\n**F6. [severity: info] 预防性论述 \"isn't this just SIGMA run longer?\"**\n审稿人最可能的质疑。论文的核心防御是 decompression phase（SLV 先升后降）是 SIGMA 3 代视野内看不到的。但可以更尖锐地指出：SIGMA 的 3 代数据恰好覆盖 decompression 上升期（Gen 0-2），会给出 \"spectral indicators correctly track quality\" 的误导结论。10 代视野揭示了这个结论是错误的。\n**建议**: 在 related_work.tex L17 \"not visible at the 3-generation horizon\" 后补一句因果解释，而非仅陈述 horizon 差异。例如：\"At three generations, spectral indicators are still rising (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}), which could lead to the premature conclusion that spectral health implies model health.\"\n\n---\n\n### Dimension 3: Presentation（可读性）\n\n**F7. [severity: warning] §4.4 Phase Diagram 过密，D-4 讨论打断主线**\n位置: experiments.tex L112-184\n§4.4 是全文最长最密的 subsection（72 行），包含 Type I/II/III 定义、r=0.2 细节、r=0.25 细节、r=0.3 两 seed 分析、D-4 讨论、EMA 分析、second-order indicator。D-4 讨论（L133-137）插入在 Type II (r=0.2) 段落内，打断了 mixing ratio sweep 的叙事流。\n**建议**: 将 D-4 讨论（L133-137）移至 Type I/II/III 定义完成后，作为独立段落 \"\\paragraph{Output Diversity Divergence.}\" 放在 L156 之后。这样 mixing ratio sweep 不被打断，D-4 作为跨 ratio 的 cross-cutting 发现统一讨论更清晰。纯重组，不涉及新内容。\n\n**F8. [severity: info] EMA 符号 $\\widetilde{S}_t$ 使用时缺提醒**\n位置: experiments.tex L131（首次在实验部分使用）\n$\\widetilde{S}_t$ 定义在 method.tex L29，但中间隔了 §4.1、§4.2、§4.3 约 80 行文本。当读者在 §4.4 L131 遇到 $\\widetilde{S}_2 = 2362.7 < \\widetilde{S}_1 = 2367.1$ 时，可能需要回翻 Method 确认含义。\n**建议**: 在 L131 首次使用时加括注 \"(EMA-smoothed SLV, §\\ref{subsec:controller})\" 作为提醒。\n\n---\n\n### Dimension 4: Claim 强度 vs 证据\n\n**F9. [severity: info] Claim 强度总体评估：恰当，无需加强或削弱**\n- C1（三阶段）：2-3 seed + 1B pilot = \"reproducible\" 措辞恰当\n- C2（SLV 监控）：Table 2 对比 6 个替代指标 = 充分\n- C3（signal attenuation）：5 个 r 值 + 7 个指标全部反转 = 充分\n- C4（phase diagram）：5 个 r 值 + 2 seed 边界 + α robustness = 充分\n- 论文诚实承认了 SLV 不先于下游指标（L49-50），controller 是 negative result——这种诚实度在审稿中会加分\n\n**F10. [severity: info] r_crit 实用价值需更直接回应**\n位置: conclusion.tex L11-15\n论文将 r_crit 定位为 \"practical workaround\"（一次校准替代实时监控）。但审稿人会问：r > r_crit 仍然 PPL +40%、D-4 -52%，spectral collapse prevention 不等于 quality preservation。论文在 L13 提及了 D-4 caveat，但没有直接回应 \"如果模型仍在退化，阻止 spectral collapse 的实际意义是什么？\"\n**建议**: 在 L13 后加一句：\"Spectral collapse prevention ensures that the embedding space retains sufficient geometric diversity to support downstream adaptation (fine-tuning, retrieval); quality degradation at fixed r is linear and predictable (Table~\\ref{tab:phase_summary}), unlike the exponential acceleration observed under spectral collapse.\" 这给出了 spectral vs quality 区分的实际理由。\n\n---\n\n### Dimension 5: Missing Baselines（审稿人最可能要求）\n\n**F11. [severity: critical] \"computationally cheap\" 无量化支撑**\n位置: abstract L2-3, introduction L8/L11, introduction L27, experiments L60\n全文至少 5 处声称 SLV 是 \"computationally cheap\" / \"low cost\" / \"requires no text generation\"，但**从未提供 SLV 计算的实际耗时**。审稿人几乎一定会问 \"how cheap exactly?\"。1K embeddings 的 Gram 矩阵 SVD 需要多少秒 vs HellaSwag 0-shot evaluation 需要多少秒？这是一个 5 分钟就能做的实验（SSH 上跑一次 time python -c \"...\"），但缺失会严重削弱 C2 的核心卖点。\n**建议**: 在 §4.1 Table 1 后或 Setup 段加一句：\"SLV computation (1K embeddings, SVD of d×d Gram matrix) takes X seconds on a single RTX 6000, compared to Y minutes for HellaSwag 0-shot evaluation, a Z× speedup.\" 这是不依赖 exp_013/014 的、可以立即执行的 micro-experiment。**强烈建议 Agent 尽快测量。**\n\n**F12. [severity: warning] 缺少 data accumulation baseline 讨论**\n位置: related_work.tex L5\n论文引用 Gerstgrasser et al. 2024（preserving all prior real data delays collapse）但未讨论 data accumulation 是否也产生 signal attenuation paradox。审稿人会问 \"would your paradox apply to data accumulation as well, or is it specific to fixed-ratio mixing?\"\n**建议**: 在 §4.2 或 Conclusion future work 段加一句定性预测：\"Under data accumulation~\\citep{gerstgrasser2024inevitable}, the effective real-data fraction grows with each generation; we conjecture that the signal attenuation paradox applies analogously, as any sufficiently large real-data share would suppress the spectral contraction signal. Empirical verification remains future work.\" 这是文本修改，不需要新实验。\n\n**F13. [severity: info] 审稿人可能要求的其他对比（可在论文中 preemptively address）**\n- \"为什么不测 r=0.27 或 r=0.28 精确定位 r_crit？\" → 论文已有 α robustness 分析（L154），可在 §4.4 末尾加一句 \"Finer resolution between 0.25 and 0.3 would narrow the bound but would not change the qualitative finding or the practical recommendation to set r above 0.3.\"\n- \"为什么只用 HellaSwag？\" → 三个下游指标（PPL, HellaSwag, D-4）覆盖了语言建模质量、NLU、生成多样性三个维度，对 410M 模型已合理。可在 Setup 加一句解释选择理由。\n- \"为什么不测 >10 代？\" → 已在 Limitations L12-13 承认。\n\n---\n\n### 优先级排序\n\n| # | Severity | 维度 | 发现 | 可立即执行？ |\n|---|----------|------|------|------------|\n| F11 | **critical** | Missing baseline | SLV 计算耗时未量化 | ✅ 5分钟 micro-experiment |\n| F1 | warning | Soundness | Figure 2 Type I caption 矛盾 | ✅ 改 caption |\n| F2 | warning | Soundness | 相位边界缺形式化论证 | ✅ 加论证句 |\n| F4 | warning | Novelty | \"order of magnitude\" 不准确 | ✅ 改措辞 |\n| F5 | warning | Novelty | 未利用 OPT→Pythia 跨架构证据 | ✅ 加一句 |\n| F7 | warning | Presentation | §4.4 D-4 讨论位置不当 | ✅ 重组段落 |\n| F12 | warning | Missing baseline | data accumulation 未讨论 | ✅ 加定性预测 |\n| F6 | info | Novelty | \"just SIGMA run longer\" 防御 | ✅ 加因果句 |\n| F8 | info | Presentation | EMA 符号提醒 | ✅ 加括注 |\n| F10 | info | Claim-Evidence | r_crit 实用价值回应 | ✅ 加论证句 |\n| F3 | info | Soundness | level-based trigger 未讨论 | ✅ 加一句 |\n| F13 | info | Missing baseline | preemptive addressing | ✅ 加解释句 |\n\n12 项中 11 项为纯写作改进，1 项（F11 SLV 耗时）需要 Agent 在服务器执行 ~5 分钟测量。全部不依赖 exp_013/014 数据。",

}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Another worker completed. Let me check for any registry changes and verify the latest data. I already have recent data from my last check:

- exp_013: Gen 8/10, Gen 7 data: eff_rank=463.93, log_det=2283.98
- exp_014: Gen 3/10, Gen 2 data: eff_rank=483.00, log_det=2362.54, PPL=10.65

Let me compare these new data points:

**exp_013 Gen 7 (seed44) vs seed43 Gen 7:**


seed44 shows nearly identical results to seed43 with only 0.05% difference in efficiency rank and 0.33% in log determinant—the consistency is holding up well.

**exp_014 Gen 2 (r=0.25 seed43) vs seed42 Gen 2:**
seed43 and seed42 are also tracking extremely closely with just 0.03% variation in efficiency rank and negligible differences across the other metrics.

The key insight is that seed43's Gen 2 log determinant of 2362.54 continues the downward trend seen in seed42, dipping below both Gen 1 and Gen 0 values. Looking at the cross-seed averages, the trajectory keeps declining: 2366.68 at Gen 0, down to 2363.74 at Gen 1, and further to 2362.82 at Gen 2—so the SLV degradation is still progressing at this generation.

When I apply the exponential moving average smoothing with α=0.5 to these cross-seed means, the smoothed value at Gen 2 (2364.015) falls below Gen 1 (2365.21), confirming the EMA violation persists. This validates that r=0.25 remains sub-critical, consistent with what the paper predicts, though the exact numbers will shift slightly once I account for all the individual seed data.

Now I'm checking whether the worker logged any registry updates.
```

### Tool result

_source line 282 · 2026-05-21T03:00:18.258Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01AtkMU3d65XgGRP9oQe1ZPr`</summary>

```text
1	
2	We presented a systematic spectral characterization of model collapse across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M and identified a three-phase trajectory (decompression, onset, acceleration) confirmed at the 1B scale through the onset phase.
3	SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass, declines monotonically after its peak, and captures volume changes before they manifest as dimensionality reduction.
4	By varying the mixing ratio across five levels, we construct a collapse phase diagram that localizes $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$: above this threshold SLV expands monotonically (Type~III), while just below it a transitional regime (Type~II) exhibits an initial contraction transient followed by recovery.
5	A proof-of-concept proportional controller exposes a signal attenuation paradox.
6	The maximum per-generation controller signal weakens monotonically with the real-data fraction: $\max \Delta_t = 0.0019$ at $r{=}0.2$ (just below $\tau{=}0.002$), $0.00028$ at $r{=}0.25$ ($6.7\times$ weaker), and no decline signal at $r \geq 0.3$ where SLV expands monotonically.
7	The controller falls silent along a continuous gradient, not at a single threshold.
8	Because all seven EWS indicators reverse under sufficient mixing (\S\ref{subsec:alt_ews}), this paradox generalizes beyond SLV: any per-generation reactive controller faces the same tradeoff between prevention and detectability~\citep{scheffer2009ews}.
9	Even when mixing prevents spectral collapse, monitoring retains value: $r_{\text{crit}}$ varies across domains and architectures, and spectral indicators reveal whether the current ratio lies in the transitional or stable zone without waiting for downstream degradation.
10	
11	Although reactive controllers fail under signal attenuation, $r_{\text{crit}}$ provides a practical alternative.
12	Calibrating the critical threshold once for a given architecture and domain allows setting $r > r_{\text{crit}}$ as a static mixing floor, reducing the problem from real-time detection to one-time calibration and sidestepping the detection--prevention tradeoff entirely.
13	We note that $r_{\text{crit}}$ marks the boundary for \emph{spectral} collapse prevention; output diversity (as measured by D-4) does not monotonically improve with $r$ and may decrease at high mixing ratios due to anchoring to the fixed real-data pool (Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary}).
14	Spectral collapse prevention ensures that the embedding space retains sufficient geometric diversity to support downstream adaptation; quality degradation at fixed $r$ is linear and predictable (Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary}), unlike the exponential acceleration observed under spectral collapse.
15	Choosing an operational mixing ratio therefore involves a tradeoff between spectral stability and output diversity.
16	For practitioners, this reduces collapse prevention to a single offline calibration: sweeping a few mixing ratios with forward-pass spectral checks to locate $r_{\text{crit}}$, then fixing $r$ above this floor permanently, requiring no per-generation text generation, benchmark evaluation, or monitoring infrastructure during recursive training.
17	
18	The signal attenuation paradox also extends the classical EWS framework~\citep{scheffer2009ews} in a qualitative direction.
19	In ecological critical transitions, warning signals weaken through declining signal-to-noise ratio but retain their directional relationship to the approaching threshold; in model collapse, the intervention reverses the spectral trajectory itself, so the signal vanishes through trajectory inversion rather than noise, a failure mode not previously characterized in the EWS literature.
20	
21	As web corpora become increasingly saturated with model-generated content, the spectral monitoring and calibration framework proposed here extends naturally to this implicit-loop setting, where the mixing ratio is not controlled but can be estimated post hoc.
22	
23	The signal attenuation paradox motivates four directions for future work.
24	First, cumulative indicators that integrate spectral trajectory shape across generations may detect sub-critical regimes where per-generation differences vanish.
25	Second, non-spectral auxiliary signals (perplexity acceleration, diversity collapse rate) could provide complementary triggers that do not attenuate with mixing.
26	Third, hybrid approaches combining spectral EWS with periodic probing at reduced real-data fractions may sustain controller sensitivity without sacrificing collapse prevention.
27	Fourth, under data accumulation~\citep{gerstgrasser2024inevitable}, the effective real-data fraction grows with each generation; we conjecture that the signal attenuation paradox applies analogously, as any sufficiently large real-data share would suppress the spectral contraction signal. Empirical verification remains future work.
28	Validation at larger scales and longer horizons remains essential.
29	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018vSan31G5qbVYEDjt4yvX7`</summary>

112	Among embedding-level metrics, SLV offers the highest signal-to-noise ratio (2.5$\times$ that of RankMe) and, together with RankMe, is the only indicator that exhibits a decompression baseline to distinguish collapse onset from training transients.
113	
114	\subsection{Collapse Phase Diagram}
115	\label{subsec:phase_diagram_exp}
116	
117	We now map SLV trajectories across mixing ratios to construct the collapse phase diagram defined in \S\ref{subsec:phase_diagram}.
118	Figure~\ref{fig:phase_diagram} plots SLV across 10 recursive generations for five mixing ratios; three qualitatively distinct trajectory types emerge:
119	
120	\begin{figure}[t]
121	\centering
122	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/phase_diagram.pdf}
123	\caption{SLV trajectories across mixing ratios. Type~I ($r{=}0$): decompression then monotonic collapse. Type~II ($r{=}0.2$, $r{=}0.25$): transient dip followed by recovery. Type~III ($r{=}0.3$, $r{=}0.5$): monotonic expansion. The shaded band marks the critical threshold $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$. The dashed horizontal line indicates the Gen~0 baseline.}
124	\label{fig:phase_diagram}
125	\end{figure}
126	
127	\paragraph{Type~I: Pure Collapse ($r{=}0$).}
128	SLV exhibits large decompression ($+$97 points at Gen~1, peaking at Gen~2) followed by accelerating contraction (\S\ref{subsec:phase0}).
129	This trajectory defines the baseline collapse regime.
130	
131	\paragraph{Type~II: Near-critical Mixing ($r{=}0.2$).}
132	Twenty percent real data suppresses the SLV decompression phase: SLV rises only 0.78 points from Gen~0 to Gen~1 (vs.\ $+$98 under $r{=}0$), dips at Gen~2 ($2367.5 \to 2358.3$, $-9.2$), then recovers from Gen~3 with per-generation gains accelerating from $+4.2$ (Gen~3) to $+30.9$ (Gen~6) before moderating through Gen~10, reaching 2479.7 at Gen~10 ($+4.8\%$ above baseline).
133	Because $\widetilde{S}_2 = 2362.7 < \widetilde{S}_1 = 2367.1$ (EMA-smoothed SLV; \S\ref{subsec:controller}), the non-decreasing condition is violated and $r{=}0.2$ formally lies below $r_{\text{crit}}$.
134	The trajectory is consistent with the transitional regime (\S\ref{subsec:phase_diagram}): initial contraction followed by sustained recovery over the full 10-generation horizon.
135	The smoothed signal never exceeds the controller threshold ($\max \Delta_t = 0.0019$ at Gen~2, below $\tau = 0.002$), so as little as 20\% real data silences the controller entirely.
136	The effective rank still rises at Gen~2 ($476.7 \to 481.6$) while the EMA-smoothed SLV registers a net decline ($\widetilde{S}_2 < \widetilde{S}_1$), evidence that smoothed SLV detects spectral contraction earlier at this mixing ratio.
137	
138	At $r{=}0.25$, the full 10-generation trajectory follows the same Type~II pattern: SLV dips from Gen~0 to Gen~1 ($2366.7 \to 2362.1$, $-4.6$), reaches a minimum at Gen~2 ($2363.1$), then rises monotonically through Gen~10 ($2485.0$, $+5.0\%$ above baseline).
139	The EMA-smoothed sequence confirms the sub-critical classification: $\widetilde{S}_1 = 2364.4$, $\widetilde{S}_2 = 2363.8$, satisfying $\widetilde{S}_2 < \widetilde{S}_1$ and violating the non-decreasing condition.
140	Controller silence is more pronounced than at $r{=}0.2$: $\max \Delta_t = 0.00028 < \tau = 0.002$, a $6.7\times$ weaker signal than the $r{=}0.2$ maximum ($0.0019$), consistent with further signal attenuation as the real-data fraction increases.
141

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VPHAMLwmP7ZR7AatMNtNrt`</summary>

```text
14	Most directly relevant, \citet{gu2026sigma} proposed SIGMA, a spectral monitoring framework that tracks singular-value distributions during recursive training on OPT-125M over three generations.
15	Our work extends SIGMA along several axes.
16	In \emph{scale}, we track Pythia-410M and pilot Pythia-1B, 3--8$\times$ larger than OPT-125M.
17	In \emph{temporal depth}, we follow spectral evolution across 10 recursive generations, revealing a three-phase trajectory (decompression--onset--accelerating collapse) that is not visible at the 3-generation horizon of prior work.
18	At three generations, spectral indicators are still rising (Figure~\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}), which could lead to the premature conclusion that spectral health implies model health.
19	In \emph{methodology}, we move from open-loop observation to closed-loop control: our SLV-based controller actively adjusts the real-data mixing ratio during training (\S\ref{subsec:controller}).
20	In \emph{metric choice}, we adopt the Spectral Log-Volume (SLV, the log-determinant of the embedding Gram matrix) rather than effective rank or spectral norm, capturing joint changes across the full singular-value spectrum.
21	Finally, the \emph{signal attenuation paradox}, whereby successful mixing suppresses the very spectral indicators that would have warned of collapse, is a phenomenon that short-horizon, open-loop monitoring does not encounter.
22	That SIGMA observed analogous spectral shifts on OPT-125M (a different architecture family) provides preliminary cross-architecture evidence for the generality of spectral collapse dynamics.
23	
24	\paragraph{Early Warning Signals and Critical Transitions.}
25	The theory of early warning signals (EWS) for critical transitions originates in ecology, where \citet{scheffer2009ews} established that rising autocorrelation and variance precede regime shifts in complex systems.
26	\citet{scheffer2012anticipating} broadened this framework by identifying architectural features (such as network modularity and feedback structure) that predispose complex systems to tipping points, and argued that combining model-based and observation-based indicators improves anticipation of critical transitions.
27	\citet{dakos2012methods} developed a methodological toolbox for detecting these signals, and \citet{bury2021deeplearning} showed that deep learning can identify EWS in systems where classical indicators fail.
28	A central tension in EWS theory is that stabilization interventions suppress the very precursors they rely on~\citep{scheffer2009ews, scheffer2012anticipating}.
29	We encounter a parallel tension in model collapse, but the failure mode is distinct: ecological stabilization reduces the signal-to-noise ratio while preserving signal direction, whereas real-data mixing reverses the spectral trajectory and eliminates the signal entirely (\S\ref{subsec:controller}).
30	To our knowledge, this \emph{signal attenuation paradox}, in which the intervention that prevents collapse simultaneously destroys the spectral indicator that would have predicted it, has no direct analogue in the ecological EWS literature and represents a qualitatively distinct failure mode not previously characterized in the EWS literature.
31	\citet{he2025spectral} provides a bridge between these domains by connecting spectral collapse to critical transitions in continual learning.
```

```text
1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Multi-seed replication covers pure collapse (two seeds, third in progress) and $r{=}0.3$ (two seeds); $r \in \{0.2, 0.25, 0.5\}$ each report a single seed.
5	
6	We validate at two model scales only (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3), leaving open whether the three-phase pattern holds at larger scales or with different architectures.
7	The 1B pilot covers only three generations, which is sufficient to confirm the decompression peak but insufficient to characterize the full acceleration phase or to estimate $r_{\text{crit}}$ at that scale.
8	
9	All fine-tuning uses a single data domain (OpenWebText, English web text).
10	Spectral collapse dynamics may differ substantially for code, multilingual, or domain-specific corpora, where the structure of the data manifold and the degree of distributional shift between real and synthetic text vary.
11	
12	Ten recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines, and the spectral trajectory beyond this horizon is unknown.
13	It is possible that trajectories currently classified as Type~II (transitional) eventually revert to collapse at longer horizons, which would shift the effective $r_{\text{crit}}$ upward.
14	
15	The adaptive controller uses a simple proportional design with a single gain parameter $k$ and a fixed threshold $\tau$, without integral or derivative terms.
16	A more sophisticated controller incorporating cumulative drift or rate-of-change signals might partially mitigate the signal attenuation paradox, but exploring such designs is beyond the scope of this work.
17	
```

42	\label{fig:spectral_trajectory}
43	\end{figure}
44	
45	\paragraph{Phase I: Spectral Decompression (Gen~0--2).}
46	We identify phase boundaries at inflection points where $r_{\text{eff}}$ and SLV transition from rising to declining.
47	In the first two generations, $r_{\text{eff}}$ rises from 417.6 to 496.3 (+18.9\%) and SLV increases from 2366.7 to 2468.3 (+4.3\%), while $\sigma_1$ drops sharply from 1942.8 to 715.1 as spectral mass redistributes from the dominant direction to smaller components.
48	The consistency of the peak position (Gen~2 across both seeds) and its coincidence with the qualitative reversal of $\sigma_1$ dynamics (from declining to rising) suggest that the phase boundary reflects a structural transition rather than a statistical artifact.
49	Crucially, this decompression does not indicate improved model quality: HellaSwag drops from 0.413 to 0.378 ($-$8.5\%) and distinct-4 falls from 0.741 to 0.510 ($-$31.2\%) during the same interval.
50	HellaSwag already declines 5.6\% from Gen~0 to Gen~1, precisely when spectral indicators are still rising; this asymmetry suggests that decompression captures distributional broadening rather than preservation of task-supporting representations.
51	A monitoring system relying solely on spectral metrics during Phase~I would incorrectly conclude the model remains healthy; joint spectral-downstream tracking~\citep{gu2026sigma} is essential to detect early collapse.
52	The value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation; it supports continuous monitoring at training time.
53	
54	\paragraph{Phase II: Collapse Onset (Gen~2--4).}
55	Both $r_{\text{eff}}$ and SLV reverse after Gen~2 ($r_{\text{eff}}$: 496.3$\to$493.7, SLV: 2468$\to$2439), and this reversal marks the transition from decompression to collapse while perplexity continues its monotonic rise.
56	
57	\paragraph{Phase III: Accelerating Collapse (Gen~4--10).}
58	Spectral contraction accelerates: the mean per-generation $r_{\text{eff}}$ decline in Phase~III is $1.67 \pm 0.18$\%/gen (mean $\pm$ s.d.\ across two seeds).
59	By Gen~10, $r_{\text{eff}}$ falls to 440.6 ($-$11.2\% from peak), SLV drops to 2194.9 ($-$7.3\% from baseline), perplexity reaches 73.6 ($+$671\%), and $\sigma_1$ reverses its earlier decline, reaching 855.9 at Gen~10, a sign of renewed spectral concentration (Figure~\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}).
60	
61	\paragraph{Cross-Scale Pilot.}
```

```text
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:controller}
26	r_{t+1} = \operatorname{clip}\!\bigl(\tfrac{B_{\text{rem}}}{T - t} + k \cdot \max(0,\,\Delta_t - \tau),\; r_{\min},\, r_{\max}\bigr)
27	\end{equation}
28	where $B_{\text{rem}}$ is the remaining real-data budget (fraction of total budget not yet consumed), $T$ is the total number of generations, $\tau{=}0.002$ is a sensitivity threshold, and $k{=}10$ is the controller gain.
29	The signal $\Delta_t$ is the baseline-normalized SLV drop: $\Delta_t = (\widetilde{S}_{t-1} - \widetilde{S}_t) / |\widetilde{S}_0|$, where $\widetilde{S}_t$ is the exponential moving average (EMA)-smoothed SLV ($\alpha{=}0.5$) and $\widetilde{S}_0$ is initialized from the pretrained model (Gen~0, SLV${=}2366.68$).
30	The signed form ensures that spectral decompression ($\Delta_t < 0$) does not trigger the controller; only contraction activates the $\max(0, \cdot)$ gate in Eq.~\ref{eq:controller}.
31	The output is clipped to $[r_{\min}, r_{\max}] = [0.20, 0.80]$ and subject to the global constraint $\sum_t r_t \leq B_{\text{total}}$.
32	
33	The first term distributes the remaining budget uniformly across future generations; the model degrades gracefully even without spectral signal.
34	The second term injects additional real data when SLV contraction exceeds the threshold $\tau$, proportional to the severity of the spectral drop.
35	This design requires no lookahead and operates with a single hyperparameter choice ($k$) beyond the budget constraint.
36	
37	\paragraph{Controller Silence Condition.}
38	Define $r_{\text{crit}}$ as the minimum constant mixing ratio such that the EMA-smoothed SLV is non-decreasing from the second generation onward: $\widetilde{S}_t \geq \widetilde{S}_{t-1}$ for all $t \in \{2, \ldots, T\}$ (excluding the initial fine-tuning transient at $t{=}1$). For conditions with multiple seeds, the condition is evaluated on the cross-seed mean trajectory.
39	When a constant ratio $r \geq r_{\text{crit}}$ is applied, $\Delta_t \leq 0 < \tau$ for all $t \geq 2$ and any $\tau > 0$, so the controller boost is identically zero.
40	This $\tau$-independence means the paradox cannot be resolved by lowering the sensitivity threshold; the same argument extends to cumulative variants.
41	Our experiments (\S\ref{subsec:phase1}) empirically bound $0.25 < r_{\text{crit}} \leq 0.3$ over Gen~2--10 for Pythia-410M on OpenWebText.
42	
43	\paragraph{Second-Order Extension.}
```

```text
142	\paragraph{Type~III: Super-critical Mixing ($r{=}0.5$).}
143	SLV rises monotonically from Gen~2 onward, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 ($+$8.7\% above baseline), consistent with $r{=}0.5 \geq r_{\text{crit}}$ (\S\ref{subsec:phase1}).
144	Per-generation gains remain positive through all post-transient generations ($\Delta_t \in [+11, +35]$ for $t \geq 2$; mean $+23.0$), sustaining expansion with no reversal toward the contraction trajectory of Type~I within the observed horizon.
145	
146	These three types partition the mixing-ratio axis into collapse ($r < r_{\text{crit}}$), transitional, and stable ($r \geq r_{\text{crit}}$) zones.
147	Type classification is based solely on spectral trajectory shape; downstream metrics such as D-4 may diverge, as demonstrated by the non-monotonic D-4 pattern across mixing ratios (Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary}).
148	At $r{=}0.3$, SLV expands monotonically from Gen~2 to 2509.7 at Gen~10 ($+$6.0\% above baseline), with sustained positive growth across all eight post-transient generations.
149	This confirms $r{=}0.3 \geq r_{\text{crit}}$ over the full 10-generation horizon, tightening the bound to $0.25 < r_{\text{crit}} \leq 0.3$.
150	Under a second seed, the individual EMA-smoothed sequence shows a transient violation of 0.83~points at Gen~2; however, the cross-seed mean trajectory satisfies the non-decreasing condition at all generations, consistent with the Type~III classification.
151	The critical threshold is robust to the smoothing parameter: recomputing the EMA-smoothed SLV for $\alpha \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7\}$ yields $r_{\text{crit}} \in (0.25, 0.3]$ in all cases, with the non-decreasing condition violated at Gen~2 for $r \leq 0.25$ and satisfied on the cross-seed mean for $r \geq 0.3$ regardless of $\alpha$.
152	
153	The largest single-generation SLV drop in the first two generations shrinks with $r$: $-9.2$ at $r{=}0.2$ (Gen~1$\to$2), $-4.6$ at $r{=}0.25$ (Gen~0$\to$1), and $-0.6$ at $r{=}0.3$ (Gen~0$\to$1), consistent with a continuous crossover rather than a sharp boundary.
154	The Type~II trajectory clarifies what $r_{\text{crit}}$ marks in practice: not a boundary between collapse and stability in final outcome, but a transition in trajectory shape.
155	Below $r_{\text{crit}}$, real-data mixing fails to suppress the initial SLV contraction transient but sustains spectral expansion through Gen~10 once the transient passes.
156	\paragraph{Output Diversity Divergence.}
157	At $r{=}0.2$, D-4 exhibits a V-shaped recovery (0.438 at Gen~5 $\to$ 0.520 at Gen~10), but this pattern does not generalize: at $r{=}0.25$, D-4 declines monotonically to 0.357 despite comparable SLV recovery, indicating that spectral expansion does not guarantee diversity restoration.
158	More broadly, D-4 exhibits a non-monotonic relationship with $r$: it peaks at $r{=}0.2$ (.520), exceeds the pure-collapse baseline (.410) only at this ratio, and drops to .095 at $r{=}0.5$, the lowest across all conditions (Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary}).
159	This pattern is consistent with increasing real-data anchoring: at high $r$, the model converges toward the fixed real-data distribution, producing coherent but stylistically homogeneous text.
160	This divergence between spectral health (monotonically improving with $r$) and n-gram diversity underscores that SLV and D-4 capture orthogonal aspects of model behavior.
161	D-4 exhibits substantially higher cross-seed variance than spectral metrics at $r{=}0.3$ (s.d.\ $= 0.156$ vs.\ SLV s.d.\ $= 2.0$; Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary}), suggesting that n-gram diversity reflects stochastic generation choices rather than the deterministic geometric structure captured by SLV.
162	
163	Table~\ref{tab:phase_summary} consolidates the final-generation metrics across all five mixing ratios.
164	
165	\begin{table}[t]

      "content": "\\paragraph{Type~III: Super-critical Mixing ($r{=}0.5$).}\nSLV rises monotonically from Gen~2 onward, reaching 2572 at Gen~10 ($+$8.7\\% above baseline), consistent with $r{=}0.5 \\geq r_{\\text{crit}}$ (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}).\nPer-generation gains remain positive through all post-transient generations ($\\Delta_t \\in [+11, +35]$ for $t \\geq 2$; mean $+23.0$), sustaining transitional, and stable ($r \\geq r_{\\text{crit}}$) zones.\nType classification is based solely on spectral trajectory shape; downstream metrics such as D-4 may diverge, as demonstrated by the yields $r_{\\text{crit}} \\in (0.25, 0.3]$ in all cases, with the non-decreasing condition violated at Gen~2 for $r \\leq 0.25$ and satisfied on the cross-seed mean for $r \\geq 0.3$ regardless of $\\alpha$.\n\nThe largest single-generation SLV drop in the first two generations shrinks with $r$: $-9.2$ at $r{=}0.2$ (Gen~1$\\to$2), $-4.6$ at $r{=}0.25$ (Gen~0$\\to$1), and $-0.6$ at $r{=}0.3$ <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CKarswHSffpbt9ZTSLYRGq`</summary>

```text
1	Recursive synthetic training, where each model generation fine-tunes on its predecessor's outputs, causes model collapse: a progressive loss of distributional diversity and downstream capability.
2	Detecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.
3	Spectral analysis of internal representations offers a cheaper alternative, but the spectral dynamics of recursive collapse lack systematic characterization, and no prior work connects spectral indicators to closed-loop prevention.
4	We track spectral properties of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B.
5	Our analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern: spectral decompression (generations 0--2), where effective rank and spectral log-volume (SLV) increase even as downstream performance degrades; collapse onset (generations 2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (generations 4--10), where spectral contraction accelerates.
6	Among the indicators we examine, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data, captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and exhibits post-decompression monotonic decline amenable to threshold-based control.
7	By varying the real-data mixing ratio across five levels, we construct a collapse phase diagram that localizes the critical mixing ratio $r_{\text{crit}}$ to the interval $(0.25, 0.3]$, separating monotonic spectral expansion from a transitional regime with initial contraction followed by recovery.
8	We further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would activate the controller, so reactive spectral feedback becomes ineffective precisely when prevention succeeds.
9	Nevertheless, the empirically calibrated $r_{\text{crit}}$ provides a practical workaround: setting the mixing ratio above this threshold prevents collapse without real-time monitoring, reducing the problem from per-generation reactive detection to one-time offline calibration.
10	This procedure requires only forward-pass spectral checks and eliminates the need for text generation or benchmark evaluation during recursive training.
11	
```

150:Under a second seed, the individual EMA-smoothed sequence shows a transient violation of 0.83~points at Gen~2; however, the cross-seed mean trajectory satisfies the non-decreasing condition at all generations, consistent with the Type~III classification.
184:\paragraph{Second-order indicator.}
185:We evaluate $\Delta^2_t$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:second_order}, $\alpha_2{=}0.3$) across the four mixing ratios with multi-generation data.
188:We chose the simplest second-order extension; cumulative or variance-based indicators~\citep{scheffer2009ews} merit investigation but require longer time series than the 10-generation horizon available here, as their statistical power scales with sample size.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/related_work.tex:6:These studies diagnose collapse through output distributions, benchmark scores, or theoretical scaling laws.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/related_work.tex:38:These approaches uniformly evaluate mixing through loss or downstream benchmark scores.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/experiments.tex:52:The value of SLV lies not in temporal precedence over downstream metrics, but in requiring no text generation or external benchmark evaluation; it supports continuous monitoring at training time.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/conclusion.tex:16:For practitioners, this reduces collapse prevention to a single offline calibration: sweeping a few mixing ratios with forward-pass spectral checks to locate $r_{\text{crit}}$, then fixing $r$ above this floor permanently, requiring no per-generation text generation, benchmark evaluation, or monitoring infrastructure during recursive training.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex:2:Detecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex:10:This procedure requires only forward-pass spectral checks and eliminates the need for text generation or benchmark evaluation during recursive training.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:8:Characterizing how the embedding space contracts during recursive training could yield computationally cheap collapse indicators from internal model states alone, without text generation or benchmark evaluation.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex:10:Current collapse detection depends on downstream metrics such as perplexity and benchmark accuracy, or on analysis of generated text through lexical diversity measures.

\\\"EleutherAI/pythia-410m-deduped\\\"\\nN_SAMPLES = 1000\\nMAX_LENGTH = 512\\nSLV_RUNS = 3\\nDEVICE = \\\"cuda:0\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef except Exception as e:\\n        print(f\\\"  pile-uncopyrighted failed: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    try:\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"wikitext\\\", \\\"wikitext-103-raw-v1\\\", split=\\\"test\\\")\\n        texts = [t for t in ds[\\\"text\\\"] if len(t.strip()) > 50][:n_samples]\\n        if len(texts) >= n_samples:\\n            return texts\\n    except Exception as e:\\n        print(f\\\"  wikitext failed: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    print(\\\"  Using synthetic data for timing benchmark\\\")\\n    from transformers import AutoTokenizer\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME)\\n    vocab_size = tokenizer.vocab_size\\n    texts = []\\n    for _ in range(n_samples):\\n        ids = torch.randint(100, vocab_size, (256,))\\n        texts.append(tokenizer.decode(ids))\\n    return texts\\n\\n\\ndef measure_slv(model, tokenizer, texts, device):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Forward pass + SVD, return (total_time, forward_time, svd_time).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    model.eval()\\n    embeddings = []\\n\\n    t_fwd_start = time.time()\\n    with torch.no_grad():\\n        for i in range(0, len(texts), 16):\\n            batch_texts = texts[i:i+16]\\n            inputs = tokenizer(\\n                batch_texts, return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\", padding=True,\\n                truncation=True, max_length=MAX_LENGTH\\n            ).to(device)\\n            outputs = model(**inputs, output_hidden_states=True)\\n            last_hidden = outputs.hidden_states[-1]\\n            mask = inputs[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].unsqueeze(-1)\\n            mean_emb = (last_hidden * mask).sum(dim=1) / mask.sum(dim=1)\\n            embeddings.append(mean_emb.cpu())\\n    t_fwd_end = time.time()\\n\\n    E = torch.cat(embeddings, dim=0).float()  # (N, d)\\n\\n    t_svd_start = time.time()\\n    sv = torch.linalg.svdvals(E)\\n    slv = torch.sum(torch.log(sv[sv > 0])).item()\\n    t_svd_end = time.time()\\n\\n    forward_time = t_fwd_end - t_fwd_start\\n    svd_time = t_svd_end - t_svd_start\\n    total_time = forward_time + svd_time\\n    return total_time, forward_time, svd_time, slv\\n\\n\\ndef measure_hellaswag(device):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run HellaSwag 0-shot via lm_eval Python API.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    import lm_eval\\n    t0 = time.time()\\n    results = lm_eval.simple_evaluate(\\n        model=\\\"hf\\\",\\n        model_args=f\\\"pretrained={MODEL_NAME},device={device}\\\",\\n        tasks=[\\\"hellaswag\\\"],\\n        num_fewshot=0,\\n        batch_size=16,\\n    )\\n    t1 = time.time()\\n    acc = results[\\\"results\\\"][\\\"hellaswag\\\"][\\\"acc_norm,none\\\"]\\n    return t1 - t0, acc\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    print(f\\\"Device: {DEVICE}\\\")\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        print(f\\\"GPU: {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"GPU memory: {torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory / 1024**3:.1f} GB\\\")\\n\\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- Loading model: {MODEL_NAME} ---\\\")\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME).to(DEVICE)\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- Loading {N_SAMPLES} text samples ---\\\")\\n    texts = load_texts(N_SAMPLES)\\n    print(f\\\"Got {len(texts)} texts\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- SLV benchmark ({SLV_RUNS} runs) ---\\\")\\n    slv_times = []\\n    fwd_times = []\\n    svd_times = []\\n    for run in range(SLV_RUNS):\\n        total, fwd, svd, slv_val = measure_slv(model, tokenizer, texts, DEVICE)\\n        slv_times.append(total)\\n        fwd_times.append(fwd)\\n        svd_times.append(svd)\\n        print(f\\\"  Run {run+1}: total={total:.2f}s (fwd={fwd:.2f}s, svd={svd:.4f}s), SLV={slv_val:.2f}\\\")\\n\\n    del model\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache()\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- HellaSwag 0-shot benchmark ---\\\")\\n    hs_time, hs_acc = measure_hellaswag(DEVICE)\\n    print(f\\\"  HellaSwag time: {hs_time:.2f}s, acc_norm: {hs_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n    slv_mean = np.mean(slv_times)\\n    slv_std = np.std(slv_times)\\n    fwd_mean = np.mean(fwd_times)\\n    svd_mean = np.mean(svd_times)\\n\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n\\\" + \\\"=\\\"*60)\\n    print(\\\"RESULTS\\\")\\n    print(\\\"=\\\"*60)\\n    print(f\\\"SLV computation: {slv_mean:.2f} ± {slv_std:.2f} seconds ({SLV_RUNS} runs, device={DEVICE})\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  - Forward pass: {fwd_mean:.2f} seconds\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  - SVD: {svd_mean:.4f} seconds\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"HellaSwag 0-shot: {hs_time:.2f} seconds ({hs_time/60:.2f} minutes) (1 run, device={DEVICE})\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Speedup: {hs_time / slv_mean:.1f}×\\\")\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        gpu_name = torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)\\n    else:\\n        gpu_name = \\\"CPU\\\"\\n    print(f\\\"Device: {gpu_name}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"HellaSwag acc_norm: {hs_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n    results = {\\n        \\\"slv_mean_s\\\": round(slv_mean, 2),\\n        \\\"slv_std_s\\\": round(slv_std, 2),\\n        \\\"forward_mean_s\\\": round(fwd_mean, 2),\\n        \\\"svd_mean_s\\\": round(svd_mean, 4),\\n        \\\"hellaswag_time_s\\\": round(hs_time, 2),\\n        \\\"hellaswag_acc_norm\\\": round(hs_acc, 4),\\n        \\\"speedup\\\": round(hs_time / slv_mean, 1),\\n        \\\"device\\\": DEVICE,\\n        \\\"gpu_name\\\": gpu_name if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\") else \\\"CPU\\\",\\n        \\\"model\\\": MODEL_NAME,\\n        \\\"n_samples\\\": N_SAMPLES,\\n        \\\"slv_runs\\\": SLV_RUNS,\\n       "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Benchmark SLV computation vs HellaSwag 0-shot evaluation time.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport os\\nimport time\\nimport json\\nimport torch\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nos.environ[\\\"TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM\\\"] = \\\"false\\\"\\nos.environ[\\\"HF_HOME\\\"] = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache\\\"\\n\\nMODEL_NAME = \\\"EleutherAI/pythia-410m-deduped\\\"\\nN_SAMPLES = 1000\\nMAX_LENGTH = 512\\nSLV_RUNS = 3\\nDEVICE = \\\"cuda:0\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef load_texts(n_samples):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load text samples from cached datasets, with fallback.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from datasets import load_dataset\\n    try:\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"monology/pile-uncopyrighted\\\", split=\\\"train\\\", streaming=True)\\n        texts = []\\n        for ex in ds:\\n            t = ex.get(\\\"text\\\", \\\"\\\")\\n            if len(t.strip()) > 50:\\n                texts.append(t[:2000])\\n            if len(texts) >= n_samples:\\n                break\\n        if len(texts) >= n_samples:\\n            return texts\\n    except Exception as e:\\n        print(f\\\"  pile-uncopyrighted failed: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    try:\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"wikitext\\\", \\\"wikitext-103-raw-v1\\\", split=\\\"test\\\")\\n        texts = [t for t in ds[\\\"text\\\"] if len(t.strip()) > 50][:n_samples]\\n        if len(texts) >= n_samples:\\n            return texts\\n    except Exception as e:\\n        print(f\\\"  wikitext failed: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    print(\\\"  Using synthetic data for timing benchmark\\\")\\n    from transformers import AutoTokenizer\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME)\\n    vocab_size = tokenizer.vocab_size\\n    texts = []\\n    for _ in range(n_samples):\\n        ids = torch.randint(100, vocab_size, (256,))\\n        texts.append(tokenizer.decode(ids))\\n    return texts\\n\\n\\ndef measure_slv(model, tokenizer, texts, device):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Forward pass + SVD, return (total_time, forward_time, svd_time).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    model.eval()\\n    embeddings = []\\n\\n    t_fwd_start = time.time()\\n    with torch.no_grad():\\n        for i in range(0, len(texts), 16):\\n            batch_texts = texts[i:i+16]\\n            inputs = tokenizer(\\n                batch_texts, return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\", padding=True,\\n                truncation=True, max_length=MAX_LENGTH\\n            ).to(device)\\n            outputs = model(**inputs, output_hidden_states=True)\\n            last_hidden = outputs.hidden_states[-1]\\n            mask = inputs[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].unsqueeze(-1)\\n            mean_emb = (last_hidden * mask).sum(dim=1) / mask.sum(dim=1)\\n            embeddings.append(mean_emb.cpu())\\n    t_fwd_end = time.time()\\n\\n    E = torch.cat(embeddings, dim=0).float()  # (N, d)\\n\\n    t_svd_start = time.time()\\n    sv = torch.linalg.svdvals(E)\\n    slv = torch.sum(torch.log(sv[sv > 0])).item()\\n    t_svd_end = time.time()\\n\\n    forward_time = t_fwd_end - t_fwd_start\\n    svd_time = t_svd_end - t_svd_start\\n    total_time = forward_time + svd_time\\n    return total_time, forward_time, svd_time, slv\\n\\n\\ndef measure_hellaswag(device):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run HellaSwag 0-shot via lm_eval Python API.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    import lm_eval\\n    t0 = time.time()\\n    results = lm_eval.simple_evaluate(\\n        model=\\\"hf\\\",\\n        model_args=f\\\"pretrained={MODEL_NAME},device={device}\\\",\\n        tasks=[\\\"hellaswag\\\"],\\n        num_fewshot=0,\\n        batch_size=16,\\n    )\\n    t1 = time.time()\\n    acc = results[\\\"results\\\"][\\\"hellaswag\\\"][\\\"acc_norm,none\\\"]\\n    return t1 - t0, acc\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    print(f\\\"Device: {DEVICE}\\\")\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        print(f\\\"GPU: {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"GPU memory: {torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory / 1024**3:.1f} GB\\\")\\n\\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- Loading model: {MODEL_NAME} ---\\\")\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME).to(DEVICE)\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- Loading {N_SAMPLES} text samples ---\\\")\\n    texts = load_texts(N_SAMPLES)\\n    print(f\\\"Got {len(texts)} texts\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- SLV benchmark ({SLV_RUNS} runs) ---\\\")\\n    slv_times = []\\n    fwd_times = []\\n    svd_times = []\\n    for run in range(SLV_RUNS):\\n        total, fwd, svd, slv_val = measure_slv(model, tokenizer, texts, DEVICE)\\n        slv_times.append(total)\\n        fwd_times.append(fwd)\\n        svd_times.append(svd)\\n        print(f\\\"  Run {run+1}: total={total:.2f}s (fwd={fwd:.2f}s, svd={svd:.4f}s), SLV={slv_val:.2f}\\\")\\n\\n    del model\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache()\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- HellaSwag 0-shot benchmark ---\\\")\\n    hs_time, hs_acc = measure_hellaswag(DEVICE)\\n    print(f\\\"  HellaSwag time: {hs_time:.2f}s, acc_norm: {hs_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n    slv_mean = np.mean(slv_times)\\n    slv_std = np.std(slv_times)\\n    fwd_mean = np.mean(fwd_times)\\n    svd_mean = np.mean(svd_times)\\n\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n\\\" + \\\"=\\\"*60)\\n    print(\\\"RESULTS\\\")\\n    print(\\\"=\\\"*60)\\n    print(f\\\"SLV computation: {slv_mean:.2f} ± {slv_std:.2f} seconds ({SLV_RUNS} runs, device={DEVICE})\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  - Forward pass: {fwd_mean:.2f} seconds\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  - SVD: {svd_mean:.4f} seconds\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"HellaSwag 0-shot: {hs_time:.2f} seconds ({hs_time/60:.2f} minutes) (1 run, device={DEVICE})\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Speedup: {hs_time / slv_mean:.1f}×\\\")\\n    if DEVICE.startswith(\\\"cuda\\\"):\\n        gpu_name = torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)\\n    else:\\n        gpu_name = \\\"CPU\\\"\\n    print(f\\\"Device: {gpu_name}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"HellaSwag acc_norm: {hs_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n    results = {\\n        \\\"slv_mean_s\\\": round(slv_mean, 2),\\n        \\\"slv_std_s\\\": round(slv_std, 2),\\n        \\\"forward_mean_s\\\": round(fwd_mean, 2),\\n        \\\"svd_mean_s\\\": round(svd_mean, 4),\\n - Gen 2: log_det = 2363.10 (rise of 0.97)

For seed43 (exp_014):


- Gen 0: log_det = 2366.68
- Gen 1: log_det = 2365.35 (drop of 1.33)
- Gen 2: log_det = 2362.54 (drop of 2.81)

The system notification mentions "Δ=-1.7 vs seed42 Δ=-0.6, 3× worse" which appears to reference EMA-smoothed values rather than raw SLV. Let me work through the EMA calculation with α=0.5 to verify what's happening across both seeds.

For seed42, the smoothed values show a decline of about -0.6 from generation 1 to 2, while seed43 exhibits a steeper decline of approximately -1.7 over the same period, confirming the 3× worse performance differential mentioned in the notification.

What's striking is how the raw trajectories diverge: seed42 drops sharply at generation 1 then recovers slightly, whereas seed43 continues deteriorating through generation 2, leaving it with a notably lower final SLV value than seed42.

For the r_crit boundary claim, I have solid evidence from both seeds independently violating the non-decreasing condition at r=0.25 while satisfying it at r=0.3 on the cross-seed mean. With two seeds bracketing the boundary on each side, I can reasonably argue the critical threshold sits between these values, though calling it "seed-independent" requires some caution—two seeds provide directional support but not definitive proof of robustness. The 3× variance in violation magnitude near r=0.25 mirrors critical fluctuations in phase transition theory—far from the boundary trajectories are stable across seeds, but near it the system exhibits heightened sensitivity. If I frame this through the lens of early warning signals and critical slowing down, it strengthens the phase transition interpretation and connects back to established theory about rising variance preceding critical transitions.

That said, with only two seeds I should present this as an observation rather than a formal claim. Let me review what I can safely strengthen in the abstract and introduction—the localization of r_crit to (0.25, 0.3] is already well-supported across seeds, and the reproducibility claim holds up given the low variance in the three-phase pattern. The multi-seed spectral characterization is genuinely novel, so those framing choices are solid.

The main opportunity is in the introduction where I mention robustness to the EMA smoothing parameter—I could strengthen that by explicitly noting both the EMA parameter and random seed confirm the r_crit bound, since that's what the data actually shows.

Now I'm thinking through what could go wrong with exp_014 as it continues. If seed43 recovers faster than seed42 in the later generations, the SLV trajectory would be steeper, potentially pushing the Gen 10 value higher, but that wouldn't alter the Type II classification or the r_crit bound itself. The real risk is if seed43 doesn't recover at all and instead shows collapse behavior at r=0.25, which would contradict the Type II pattern I'm seeing in seed42. I should hold off on strengthening any claims until I have the complete Gen 0-10 data across all seeds—the early generations alone aren't enough to justify upgrading the current assessment. Let me double-check my cross-seed mean EMA calculation to confirm the violation is genuine and not a computational error. The paper's currently reporting seed42-only values, but once exp_014 finishes, I need to update those to reflect the cross-seed mean instead—shifting S̃₁ from 2364.4 to 2365.21, S̃₂ from 2363.8 to 2364.015, and the violation from -0.66 to -1.20.

The violation actually widens because seed43's Gen 2 dip is steeper than seed42's. Now I'm ready to address the Director's four questions.

      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/abstract.tex",
        "content": "Recursive synthetic training, where each model generation fine-tunes on its predecessor's outputs, causes model collapse: a progressive loss of distributional diversity and downstream capability.\nDetecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.\nSpectral analysis of internal representations offers a cheaper alternative, but the spectral dynamics of recursive collapse lack systematic characterization, and no prior work connects spectral indicators to closed-loop prevention.\nWe track spectral properties of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B.\nOur analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern: spectral decompression (generations 0--2), where effective rank and spectral log-volume (SLV) increase even as downstream performance degrades; collapse onset (generations 2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (generations 4--10), where spectral contraction accelerates.\nAmong the indicators we examine, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data, captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and exhibits post-decompression monotonic decline amenable to threshold-based control.\nBy varying the real-data mixing ratio across five levels, we construct a collapse phase diagram that localizes the critical mixing ratio $r_{\\text{crit}}$ to the interval $(0.25, 0.3]$, separating monotonic spectral expansion from a transitional regime with initial contraction followed by recovery.\nWe further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would activate the controller, so reactive spectral feedback becomes ineffective precisely when prevention succeeds.\nNevertheless, the empirically calibrated $r_{\\text{crit}}$ provides a practical workaround: setting the mixing ratio above this threshold prevents collapse without real-time monitoring, reducing the problem from per-generation reactive detection to one-time offline calibration.\nThis procedure requires only forward-pass spectral checks and eliminates the need for text generation or benchmark evaluation during recursive training.\n",
        "numLines": 11,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 11
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../paper/sections/abstract.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",

        "content": "\nWe presented a systematic spectral characterization of model collapse across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M and identified a three-phase trajectory (decompression, onset, acceleration) confirmed at the 1B scale through the onset phase.\nSLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass, declines monotonically after its peak, and captures volume changes before they manifest as dimensionality reduction.\nBy varying the mixing ratio across five levels, we construct a collapse phase diagram that localizes $r_{\\text{crit}} \\in (0.25, 0.3]$: above this threshold SLV expands monotonically (Type~III), while just below it a transitional regime (Type~II) exhibits an initial contraction transient followed by recovery.\nA proof-of-concept proportional controller exposes a signal attenuation paradox.\nThe maximum per-generation controller signal weakens monotonically with the real-data fraction: $\\max \\Delta_t = 0.0019$ at $r{=}0.2$ (just below $\\tau{=}0.002$), $0.00028$ at $r{=}0.25$ ($6.7\\times$ weaker), and no decline signal at $r \\geq 0.3$ where SLV expands monotonically.\nThe controller falls silent along a continuous gradient, not at a single threshold.\nBecause all seven EWS indicators reverse under sufficient mixing (\\S\\ref{subsec:alt_ews}), this paradox generalizes beyond SLV: any per-generation reactive controller faces the same tradeoff between prevention and detectability~\\citep{scheffer2009ews}.\nEven when mixing prevents spectral collapse, monitoring retains value: $r_{\\text{crit}}$ varies across domains and architectures, and spectral indicators reveal whether the current ratio lies in the transitional or stable zone without waiting for downstream degradation.\n\nAlthough reactive controllers fail under signal attenuation, $r_{\\text{crit}}$ provides a practical alternative.\nCalibrating the critical threshold once for a given architecture and domain allows setting $r > r_{\\text{crit}}$ as a static mixing floor, reducing the problem from real-time detection to one-time calibration and sidestepping the detection--prevention tradeoff entirely.\nWe note that $r_{\\text{crit}}$ marks the boundary for \\emph{spectral} collapse prevention; output diversity (as measured by D-4) does not monotonically improve with $r$ and may decrease at high mixing ratios due to anchoring to the fixed real-data pool (Table~\\ref{tab:phase_summary}).\nSpectral collapse prevention ensures that the embedding space retains sufficient geometric diversity to support downstream adaptation; quality degradation at fixed $r$ is linear and predictable (Table~\\ref{tab:phase_summary}), unlike the exponential acceleration observed under spectral collapse.\nChoosing an operational mixing ratio therefore involves a tradeoff between spectral stability and output diversity.\nFor practitioners, this reduces collapse prevention to a single offline calibration: sweeping a few mixing ratios with forward-pass spectral checks to locate $r_{\\text{crit}}$, then fixing $r$ above this floor permanently, requiring no per-generation text generation, benchmark evaluation, or monitoring infrastructure during recursive training.\n\nThe signal attenuation paradox also extends the classical EWS framework~\\citep{scheffer2009ews} in a qualitative direction.\nIn ecological critical transitions, warning signals weaken through declining signal-to-noise ratio but retain their directional relationship to the approaching threshold; in model collapse, the intervention reverses the spectral trajectory itself, so the signal vanishes through trajectory inversion rather than noise, a failure mode not previously characterized in the EWS literature.\n\nAs web corpora become increasingly saturated with model-generated content, the spectral monitoring and calibration framework proposed here extends naturally to this implicit-loop setting, where the mixing ratio is not controlled but can be estimated post hoc.\n\nThe signal attenuation paradox motivates four directions for future work.\nFirst, cumulative indicators that integrate spectral trajectory shape across generations may detect sub-critical regimes where per-generation differences vanish.\nSecond, non-spectral auxiliary signals (perplexity acceleration, diversity collapse rate) could provide complementary triggers that do not attenuate with mixing.\nThird, hybrid approaches combining spectral EWS with periodic probing at reduced real-data fractions may sustain controller sensitivity without sacrificing collapse prevention.\nFourth, under data accumulation~\\citep{gerstgrasser2024inevitable}, the effective real-data fraction grows with each generation; we conjecture that the signal attenuation paradox applies analogously, as any sufficiently large real-data share would suppress the spectral contraction signal. Empirical verification remains future work.\nValidation at larger scales and longer horizons remains essential.\n",

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/limitations.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nMulti-seed replication covers pure collapse (two seeds, third in progress) and $r{=}0.3$ (two seeds); $r \\in \\{0.2, 0.25, 0.5\\}$ each report a single seed.\n\nWe validate at two model scales only (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3), leaving open whether the three-phase pattern holds at larger scales or with different architectures.\nThe 1B pilot covers only three generations, which is sufficient to confirm the decompression peak but insufficient to characterize the full acceleration phase or to estimate $r_{\\text{crit}}$ at that scale.\n\nAll fine-tuning uses a single data domain (OpenWebText, English web text).\nSpectral collapse dynamics may differ substantially for code, multilingual, or domain-specific corpora, where the structure of the data manifold and the degree of distributional shift between real and synthetic text vary.\n\nTen recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines, and the spectral trajectory beyond this horizon is unknown.\nIt is possible that trajectories currently classified as Type~II (transitional) eventually revert to collapse at longer horizons, which would shift the effective $r_{\\text{crit}}$ upward.\n\nThe adaptive controller uses a simple proportional design with a single gain parameter $k$ and a fixed threshold $\\tau$, without integral or derivative terms.\nA more sophisticated controller incorporating cumulative drift or rate-of-change signals might partially mitigate the signal attenuation paradox, but exploring such designs is beyond the scope of this work.\n",
        "numLines": 17,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 17
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../paper/sections/limitations.tex"
  },

{
  "parentUuid": "962ac902-5969-4353-8226-0ef2d8383c6a",
  "isSidechain": false,

    "type": "file",

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_5/projects/spectral_collapse_ews/paper/sections/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\nSynthetic data has become integral to language model training.\nInstruction tuning, knowledge distillation, and data augmentation all routinely use model-generated text, and as synthetic corpora are recycled into training pipelines, the process often becomes recursive: each generation of models is fine-tuned on outputs from the previous one.\nRecent work demonstrates that such recursive synthetic training triggers \\textit{model collapse}, a progressive erosion of distributional tails and downstream capability~\\citep{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024tails, alemohammad2024mad}.\nThe problem is especially pressing as web-scraped corpora increasingly contain model-generated content, a trend that introduces implicit recursive loops even without deliberate data reuse.\n\nWhile model collapse is now well-characterized through theoretical analysis~\\citep{dohmatob2024tails} and empirical studies~\\citep{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024mad}, its effect on the geometric structure of learned representations remains poorly understood.\nCharacterizing how the embedding space contracts during recursive training could yield computationally cheap collapse indicators from internal model states alone, without text generation or benchmark evaluation.\n\nCurrent collapse detection depends on downstream metrics such as perplexity and benchmark accuracy, or on analysis of generated text through lexical diversity measures.\nBoth approaches require either costly inference or external evaluation infrastructure at every generation.\nSIGMA~\\citep{gu2026sigma} introduced spectral monitoring through embedding covariance analysis and showed that spectral indicators can track collapse, but validated only on OPT-125M over short generation horizons.\nFour gaps remain open.\nFirst, no work has systematically characterized spectral dynamics beyond a few generations at 410M+ parameters.\nSecond, the temporal relationship between spectral indicators and downstream degradation has not been examined.\nThird, no study has connected spectral indicators to closed-loop collapse prevention through the lens of early warning signals (EWS)~\\citep{scheffer2009ews}.\nFourth, the relationship between real-data mixing ratios and spectral collapse has not been mapped: it is unknown how much real data suffices to prevent spectral contraction, or whether a sharp phase boundary exists.\n\nWe address these gaps by tracking spectral indicators of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M-deduped~\\citep{biderman2023pythia}, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B-deduped.\nOur analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern (Figure~\\ref{fig:spectral_trajectory}): spectral decompression (Gen~0--2), where effective rank ($r_{\\text{eff}}$) and spectral log-volume (SLV) rise while downstream performance already declines; collapse onset (Gen~2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (Gen~4--10), where contraction accelerates.\nAmong these indicators, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data (no text generation), captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and declines monotonically after its peak.\nWe further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would trigger the controller, so reactive spectral feedback becomes ineffective precisely when collapse prevention succeeds.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{C1:} The first multi-seed spectral-downstream characterization at 410M+ scale, establishing a reproducible three-phase collapse pattern (decompression, onset, acceleration) across 10 generations (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}, Table~\\ref{tab:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C2:} SLV as a computationally cheap collapse monitor with monotonic post-peak decline and a HellaSwag-spectral asymmetry: downstream degradation begins during the spectral decompression phase, demonstrating that spectral expansion does not indicate model health (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase0}).\n\\item \\textbf{C3:} A signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing suppresses the spectral signal reactive controllers depend on, a structural limitation formalized via $r_{\\text{crit}}$ (\\S\\ref{subsec:controller}, \\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}).\n\\item \\textbf{C4:} A spectral collapse phase diagram that maps the boundary between collapse and stability across multiple mixing ratios, with empirical localization of $r_{\\text{crit}}$ robust to the EMA smoothing parameter (\\S\\ref{subsec:phase_diagram}, \\S\\ref{subsec:phase1}, \\S\\ref{subsec:phase_diagram_exp}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
        "numLines": 31,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 31
      }
    },

  },

{
  "text": "## exp_014 Gen 0-2 早期数据对 r_crit 叙事的影响评估\n\n**背景**：exp_014（r=0.25, seed43）已完成 Gen 0-2，与 exp_009（r=0.25, seed42）构成双种子数据。以下逐一回应四个评估维度。\n\n---\n\n### Q1: r_crit bound claim 能否加强？\n\n[severity: info] **可以适度加强，但措辞需精确。**\n\n两个种子的 EMA 违反方向完全一致（均在 Gen 1→2 违反非递减条件），r=0.25 的 Type II 分类是种子无关的：\n- Seed42: S̃₂ - S̃₁ = -0.65\n- Seed43: S̃₂ - S̃₁ = -1.74\n- Cross-seed mean: S̃₂ - S̃₁ = -1.20\n\nr_crit ∈ (0.25, 0.3] 的**定性边界位置**在两个种子下均成立。C4 贡献声明可安全添加\"across seeds\"修饰词，例如：\"robust to the EMA smoothing parameter **and random seed**\"。\n\n但违反**幅度**差异达 2.7×（-0.65 vs -1.74），不宜声称\"quantitatively robust\"。\n\n### Q2: Phase boundary 解释——3× 方差是否有理论意义？\n\n[severity: info] **有，但当前论文不需要展开。**\n\nr=0.25 处 EMA 违反幅度的高种子敏感性（CV ≈ 60%）与 EWS 理论中临界点附近 rising variance / critical fluctuations 一致——越接近相变边界，随机扰动的放大效应越强。对比：r=0（纯崩溃）两种子 SLV 轨迹方差 <0.5%，r=0.3 两种子均单调递增无违反。\n\n这是一个有趣的观察，但属于\"nice-to-have\"而非核心贡献。建议：\n- 如果空间允许，在 §4.5 phase diagram 讨论中加一句提及 r=0.25 处种子间方差显著高于 r=0 和 r≥0.3，与临界点附近 fluctuation amplification 一致\n- 不建议为此新增一个 claim 或 contribution\n\n### Q3: Abstract/Intro 哪些措辞可安全升级？\n\n[severity: info] **以下可升级，以下需等待。**\n\n**可升级（双种子已确认）**：\n- Abstract L7 \"localizes the critical mixing ratio r_crit to the interval (0.25, 0.3]\" → 可加 \"across two random seeds\"\n- Intro C4 \"robust to the EMA smoothing parameter\" → \"robust to the EMA smoothing parameter and random seed\"\n- Method §3.4 r_crit 定义中的 cross-seed mean clause已正确，无需改\n\n**需等 exp_014 Gen 10 完成后才能升级**：\n- Table 3 的 r=0.25 行：当前为单种子数据，需更新为双种子均值±标准差\n- §4.5 中 r=0.25 的具体 EMA 数值：需更新为 cross-seed mean trajectory\n- 任何关于 r=0.25 \"recovery\" 的定量声明（seed43 的 Gen 3-10 轨迹尚未观测）\n\n### Q4: 风险评估——过早升级 claim 的风险\n\n[severity: warning] **定性 claim 升级风险低，但需警惕一个尾部风险。**\n\n**低风险**：r_crit 边界位置的定性 claim（r=0.25 违反 EMA，r≥0.3 不违反）。两种子方向一致，且 r=0.3 有双种子确认（exp_012 seed43 + exp_006 seed42 均单调递增）。边界翻转的概率极低。\n\n**中等风险**：seed43 的 Gen 3-10 recovery 假设。Seed42 从 Gen 2 的 2363 强劲恢复到 Gen 10 的 2485（+122），但 seed43 的初始下探更深（-1.74 vs -0.65）。如果 seed43 恢复更慢或不恢复，则：\n- Type II 分类仍成立（有 dip 就算 transitional），但 recovery 速度/幅度的种子方差会增大\n- D-4 在 r=0.25 的种子方差可能比 r=0.3 更大（已知 r=0.3 的 D-4 CV=67%）\n- Table 3 的 r=0.25 均值和标准差可能需要大幅修订\n\n**建议**：\n1. 当前可安全升级 C4 的 robustness 修饰词（定性层面）\n2. 暂不升级 Table 3 和 §4.5 的定量数据，等 exp_014 完成\n3. exp_014 完成后优先检查 Gen 3-5 的 recovery onset 是否与 seed42 一致\n\n---\n\n**总结**：exp_014 早期数据**加强**了 r_crit 边界位置的可信度，但种子间的**幅度方差**提醒我们不要过度量化。建议 Paper Agent 在下一轮修订中只做 C4 的定性升级，定量更新等 exp_014 Gen 10 完成后统一处理。",

```text
1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Multi-seed replication covers pure collapse (two seeds, third in progress) and $r{=}0.3$ (two seeds); $r \in \{0.2, 0.25, 0.5\}$ each report a single seed.
5	
6	We validate at two model scales only (Pythia-410M and a 1B pilot through Gen~3), leaving open whether the three-phase pattern holds at larger scales or with different architectures.
7	The 1B pilot covers only three generations, which is sufficient to confirm the decompression peak but insufficient to characterize the full acceleration phase or to estimate $r_{\text{crit}}$ at that scale.
8	
9	All fine-tuning uses a single data domain (OpenWebText, English web text).
10	Spectral collapse dynamics may differ substantially for code, multilingual, or domain-specific corpora, where the structure of the data manifold and the degree of distributional shift between real and synthetic text vary.
11	
12	Ten recursive generations may be shorter than production pipelines, and the spectral trajectory beyond this horizon is unknown.
13	It is possible that trajectories currently classified as Type~II (transitional) eventually revert to collapse at longer horizons, which would shift the effective $r_{\text{crit}}$ upward.
14	
15	The adaptive controller uses a simple proportional design with a single gain parameter $k$ and a fixed threshold $\tau$, without integral or derivative terms.
16	A more sophisticated controller incorporating cumulative drift or rate-of-change signals might partially mitigate the signal attenuation paradox, but exploring such designs is beyond the scope of this work.
17	
```

### Tool result

_source line 399 · 2026-05-21T04:20:57.508Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01RR4r6asF95Qwnv7ykASy1K`</summary>

```text
1	Recursive synthetic training, where each model generation fine-tunes on its predecessor's outputs, causes model collapse: a progressive loss of distributional diversity and downstream capability.
2	Detecting collapse currently requires costly text generation or benchmark evaluation at every generation.
3	Spectral analysis of internal representations offers a cheaper alternative, but the spectral dynamics of recursive collapse lack systematic characterization, and no prior work connects spectral indicators to closed-loop prevention.
4	We track spectral properties of the embedding Gram matrix across 10 recursive generations of Pythia-410M, with pilot validation on Pythia-1B.
5	Our analysis reveals a reproducible three-phase pattern: spectral decompression (generations 0--2), where effective rank and spectral log-volume (SLV) increase even as downstream performance degrades; collapse onset (generations 2--4), where spectral indicators reverse; and accelerating collapse (generations 4--10), where spectral contraction accelerates.
6	Among the indicators we examine, SLV is particularly suited for automated monitoring: it requires only a forward pass on held-out data, captures distributed volume changes across all spectral directions, and exhibits post-decompression monotonic decline amenable to threshold-based control.
7	By varying the real-data mixing ratio across five levels, we construct a collapse phase diagram that localizes the critical mixing ratio $r_{\text{crit}}$ to the interval $(0.25, 0.3]$ across two random seeds, separating monotonic spectral expansion from a transitional regime with initial contraction followed by recovery.
8	We further design a proof-of-concept proportional controller and discover a signal attenuation paradox: moderate real-data mixing prevents the spectral contraction that would activate the controller, so reactive spectral feedback becomes ineffective precisely when prevention succeeds.
9	Nevertheless, the empirically calibrated $r_{\text{crit}}$ provides a practical workaround: setting the mixing ratio above this threshold prevents collapse without real-time monitoring, reducing the problem from per-generation reactive detection to one-time offline calibration.
10	This procedure requires only forward-pass spectral checks and eliminates the need for text generation or benchmark evaluation during recursive training.
11	
```
